# Benchmarks

This document records reproducible, black-box comparisons between rustmpeg and
the reference implementations that define the same formats. It is generated by
[`scripts/bench/bench.py`](../../scripts/bench/bench.py); the marked sections are
rewritten by CI and must not be edited by hand. The surrounding methodology and
reference table are maintained manually.

A benchmark result is evidence about one machine, one commit, one oracle
revision, and one deterministic corpus. It is not a conformance claim and it is
not a product-wide speed claim. Numbers are only compared with runs that list the
same oracle pins and the same clip hashes.

## Latest run

- Commit: `31657b58bb8bf408d5f134a4138ffd9552a992ba` (clean)

- Runner: runnervm7aw5e / Linux-7.0.0-1017-azure-x86_64-with-glibc2.39 / 4 logical CPUs / 15.6 GiB RAM
- CPU: AMD EPYC 9V74 80-Core Processor
- Rust: `rustc 1.101.0-nightly (12433c307 2026-10-24)`
- FFmpeg: `ffmpeg version 6.1.1-3ubuntu5 Copyright (c) 2000-2023 the FFmpeg developers`
- Oracles: SVT-AV1 `v4.2.0`, AVM `v1.0.0`, dav2d `d7df5418`, liblc3 `v1.1.3`, flac `flac 1.4.3`
- Sources: `graphics` sha256 `fc3488c22426424e...`, `noise` sha256 `8d279d4a27fde1dc...`

PSNR and SSIM are measured by FFmpeg against the exact source Y4M. Bitrate is `(container bytes * 8 * fps) / frame_count`; it includes the IVF/OBU container header. MB/s uses raw I420 bytes and wall-clock time for the encode or decode process only.

The rustmpeg FLAC encode time includes the example's deterministic in-process PCM synthesis, so compare decode throughput, sizes, and bit-exactness rather than its encode MB/s.

#### Not benchmarked in this run

| Item | Reason |
| :-- | :-- |
| AV2 vs AVM (noise) | AVM avmenc qp=60 exceeded the 2400s command limit |
| Matroska / ISOBMFF (format) | No runnable format benchmark binary exists and this pipeline does not modify crates; compare via the format tests instead. |
| Image codecs (JPEG XL, OpenEXR) | JPEG XL benchmark row is not wired into this workflow yet. |
| Opus | Only packet parsing and the range decoder are implemented; no encoder/decoder is available to compare. |

#### Not benchmarked in this run

| AV2 vs AVM (noise) | AVM avmenc qp=60 exceeded the 2400s command limit |

| Image codecs (JPEG XL, OpenEXR) | JPEG XL benchmark row is not wired into this workflow yet. |
| Opus | Only packet parsing and the range decoder are implemented; no encoder/decoder is available to compare. |

## What is compared

| Area | rustmpeg implementation | Reference used here | Comparable? | Notes |
| :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- |
| AV2 video | `rustmpeg-video` `av2_encode` / `av2_decode` | AVM `avmenc` (same codec) | Yes | Same-codec RD comparison; AV2 QP sweep. AVM streams are decoded with `dav2d` (`avmdec` fallback). rustmpeg streams use the native decoder first; a reference decoder is accepted only if its pixels match the encoder reconstruction, otherwise the row is labelled as a reconstruction measurement. |
| AV2 video | `rustmpeg-video` `av2_encode` / `av2_decode` | SVT-AV1 (AV1) | **No, cross-codec** | AV2 and AV1 are different standards, and the QP and CRF scales are unrelated. This row is an indicative bitrate/quality reference only and is labelled **cross-codec** in the generated table. |
| FLAC audio | `rustmpeg-audio` `flac_encode` / `flac_decode` | libFLAC (`flac`) | Yes | Both encode the exact same PCM bytes. The check is lossless and bit-exact. The rustmpeg example synthesises PCM in-process, so its encode MB/s is not a like-for-like encoder throughput number. |
| LC3 audio | `rustmpeg-audio` `lc3_encode` / `lc3_decode` | liblc3 (`elc3` / `dlc3`) | Yes | Same 48 kHz s16le PCM, 10 ms mono frames, 32/64/96 kbps. SNR is aligned over the LC3 algorithmic-delay range and the winning lag is printed. |
| Matroska / ISOBMFF | `rustmpeg-format` | FFmpeg | Not yet | The format crate has no runnable benchmark binary, and the benchmark pipeline does not modify crates to add one. Format coverage is exercised by the crate tests. |
| JPEG XL / OpenEXR | `rustmpeg-image` | — | Not yet | JPEG XL codec exists; benchmark row pending. OpenEXR is planned. |
| Opus | `rustmpeg-audio` packet parser and range decoder | libopus | Not yet | No Opus encoder or decoder exists to compare end to end. |

Conformance and correctness are separate from this document. A benchmark never
replaces a conformance vector, a bit-exact unit test, or the license audit.

## Methodology

### Deterministic content

FFmpeg generates two 30 fps, I420 8-bit clips with `testsrc2`:

- `graphics`: `testsrc2` only.
- `noise`: `testsrc2` plus seeded `noise=alls=12:allf=t+u:all_seed=1234`.

The full profile uses 1920x1080 and 10 frames. The smoke profile uses 320x240 and
4 frames, and command-line overrides allow a 64x64, 2-frame local check. The exact
FFmpeg command, frame count, and the SHA-256 prefix of each source are recorded in
the generated section. A run with different source hashes is not directly
comparable.

### Encode, decode, quality, rate

For each encoder and quality point the pipeline:

1. encodes the source Y4M;
2. decodes the bitstream with a decoder that is not the encoder under test
   (rustmpeg stream: rustmpeg decoder; AVM stream: `dav2d`; AV1 stream: FFmpeg);
3. checks that the decoded frame count equals the source frame count;
4. measures PSNR-Y and SSIM with FFmpeg against the exact source;
5. computes bitrate as `container_bytes * 8 * fps / frame_count`.

For a rustmpeg AV2 stream the native decoder runs first. If it rejects a tool
that the experimental encoder selected, `dav2d` or `avmdec` is tried, and its
pixels are accepted only if they hash-match the encoder's reconstruction. If no
decoder reproduces the reconstruction, the reconstruction itself is measured and
the row says `encoder reconstruction (native decode failed: ...)`, with decode
MB/s reported as `n/a`. This is a decoder-completeness limitation, not a hidden
decode: the table never presents reconstruction pixels as decoder output.

Bitrate includes the IVF/OBU container header. Encode and decode MB/s use wall
clock time for the encoder or decoder process and the raw I420 byte count; they
include process startup and file I/O and are not CPU-pinned. The oracles are run
single-threaded (`--threads=1`, `--lp 1`) so that the comparison is not dominated
by a different thread count.

### BD-rate

BD-rate is computed only when the two PSNR ranges overlap. Each encoder's
`log10(bitrate)` is fitted with a polynomial (degree at most three) over PSNR,
and the difference is integrated over the overlapping PSNR interval. Positive
values mean the test encoder needs more bits for the same quality; negative values
mean fewer bits. The same-codec AV2 comparison is the meaningful BD-rate. The
SVT-AV1 comparison is computed for completeness but is marked **cross-codec** and
must not be read as an AV2-vs-AV1 result.

### Oracle revisions

All oracle pins live in [`scripts/bench/oracles.env`](../../scripts/bench/oracles.env)
and are copied into every generated result:

| Oracle | Pin |
| :-- | :-- |
| SVT-AV1 | `v4.2.0` (`9292ec8e32bce26f781f277ec8739b53426c4300`) |
| AVM | `v1.0.0` (`966a7d7cd6fcf60360caf5dc413b2aeeb65e144d`) |
| dav2d | `d7df54181eed81927d7c82cf8b3e54804c3813b4` |
| liblc3 | `v1.1.3` (`96a3af0beb5487aca3b98a4b992a539a1f6d80d1`) |
| FFmpeg | distribution package recorded in the result |
| libFLAC (`flac`) | distribution package recorded in the result |

FFmpeg, libFLAC, and liblc3 are black-box oracles. Their source is not linked,
vendored, or copied into rustmpeg. AVM and SVT-AV1 are built in CI from pinned
commits and are likewise only executed as external programs.

## Reproducing

See [`scripts/bench/README.md`](../../scripts/bench/README.md) for the online and
offline commands, the oracle build steps, and the exact local dry-run.

## Related local experiments

- [`AUDIO_DIVERSITY.md`](AUDIO_DIVERSITY.md) compares seed-driven rustmpeg
  synthesis with FFmpeg `sine`, `anoisesrc`, and `aevalsrc` corpora. It is a
  local experiment with its own commands and is not part of the CI table above.

## Known limitations

- The GitHub-hosted runner is shared and not CPU-pinned. Absolute MB/s varies
  between runs; compare medians and trends, not a single run.
- AVM is reference software and is much slower than production encoders. The full
  sweep is therefore bounded to 10 frames per clip.
- AVM and dav2d track a moving AV2 draft. The pins in `oracles.env` must be updated
  together, or `dav2d` may reject an AVM bitstream from a different draft.
- The cross-codec SVT-AV1 comparison is indicative only, as explained above.
- FLAC encode throughput for the rustmpeg example includes in-process PCM
  synthesis. Decode throughput, compressed size, and bit-exactness are the
  meaningful FLAC comparisons.
- The format and image crates have no runnable benchmark harness yet. Adding one
  would require changing `crates/**`, which this pipeline deliberately does not do.
- The rustmpeg AV2 decoder does not yet accept every tool its encoder can select
  (for example sub-block transforms at larger frame sizes). Those rows fall back
  to a clearly labelled reconstruction measurement, or to an independent decoder
  only when it reproduces that reconstruction exactly. Decode throughput is `n/a`
  when the native decoder cannot parse the stream.
- A failed encode, decode, frame-count check, or quality parse fails the job and
  no partial table is published. An oracle that cannot be built or is not
  installed is skipped and listed under "Not benchmarked in this run"; it is
  never replaced by a different build or an invented number.
